- The root cause is suspected to be in how `__repr__` and `__str__` methods are implemented
- The expected behavior should show the old style with properly named multiple nodes

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Run the quickstart notebook from @miykael binder repo
2. Look at cell 14 or similar graph generation cells
3. Observe that node names are not displayed correctly in the generated diagrams

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Focus on representation methods (`__repr__`, `__str__`) 
- Ensure backward compatibility with existing graph generation
- Test with actual workflow examples
- Verify visual output correctness

    40	Current neuroimaging software offer users an incredible opportunity to
    41	analyze data using a variety of different algorithms. However, this has
    42	resulted in a heterogeneous collection of specialized applications
    43	without transparent interoperability or a uniform operating interface.
    44	
    45	*Nipype*, an open-source, community-developed initiative under the
    46	umbrella of NiPy, is a Python project that provides a uniform interface
    47	to existing neuroimaging software and facilitates interaction between
    48	these packages within a single workflow. Nipype provides an environment
    49	that encourages interactive exploration of algorithms from different
    50	packages (e.g., SPM, FSL, FreeSurfer, AFNI, Slicer, ANTS), eases the
    51	design of workflows within and between packages, and reduces the
    52	learning curve necessary to use different packages. Nipype is creating a
    53	collaborative platform for neuroimaging software development in a
    54	high-level language and addressing limitations of existing pipeline
    55	systems.
    56	
    57	*Nipype* allows you to:
    58	
    59	* easily interact with tools from different software packages
    60	* combine processing steps from different software packages
    61	* develop new workflows faster by reusing common steps from old ones
    62	* process data faster by running it in parallel on many cores/machines
    63	* make your research easily reproducible
    64	* share your processing workflows with the community
    65	
    66	Documentation
    67	-------------
    68	
    69	Please see the ``doc/README.txt`` document for information on our
    70	documentation.
    71	
    72	Website
    73	-------
    74	
    75	Information specific to Nipype is located here::
    76	
    77	    http://nipy.org/nipype
    78	
    79	
    80	Support and Communication
    81	-------------------------
    82	
    83	If you have a problem or would like to ask a question about how to do something in Nipype please open an issue to
    84	`NeuroStars.org <http://neurostars.org>`_ with a *nipype* tag. `NeuroStars.org <http://neurostars.org>`_  is a
    85	platform similar to StackOverflow but dedicated to neuroinformatics.
    86	
    87	To participate in the Nipype development related discussions please use the following mailing list::
    88	
    89	       http://mail.python.org/mailman/listinfo/neuroimaging
    90

   380	    def write_graph(self,
   381	                    dotfilename='graph.dot',
   382	                    graph2use='hierarchical',
   383	                    format="png",
   384	                    simple_form=True):
   385	        """Generates a graphviz dot file and a png file
   386	
   387	        Parameters
   388	        ----------
   389	
   390	        graph2use: 'orig', 'hierarchical' (default), 'flat', 'exec', 'colored'
   391	            orig - creates a top level graph without expanding internal
   392	            workflow nodes;
   393	            flat - expands workflow nodes recursively;
   394	            hierarchical - expands workflow nodes recursively with a
   395	            notion on hierarchy;
   396	            colored - expands workflow nodes recursively with a
   397	            notion on hierarchy in color;
   398	            exec - expands workflows to depict iterables
   399	
   400	        format: 'png', 'svg'
   401	
   402	        simple_form: boolean (default: True)
   403	            Determines if the node name used in the graph should be of the form
   404	            'nodename (package)' when True or 'nodename.Class.package' when
   405	            False.
   406	
   407	        """
   408	        graphtypes = ['orig', 'flat', 'hierarchical', 'exec', 'colored']
   409	        if graph2use not in graphtypes:
   410	            raise ValueError('Unknown graph2use keyword. Must be one of: ' +
   411	                             str(graphtypes))
   412	        base_dir, dotfilename = op.split(dotfilename)
   413	        if base_dir == '':
   414	            if self.base_dir:
   415	                base_dir = self.base_dir
   416	                if self.name:
   417	                    base_dir = op.join(base_dir, self.name)
   418	            else:
   419	                base_dir = os.getcwd()
   420	        base_dir = makedirs(base_dir, exist_ok=True)
   421	        if graph2use in ['hierarchical', 'colored']:
   422	            if self.name[:1].isdigit():  # these graphs break if int
   423	                raise ValueError('{} graph failed, workflow name cannot begin '
   424	                                 'with a number'.format(graph2use))
   425	            dotfilename = op.join(base_dir, dotfilename)
   426	            self.write_hierarchical_dotfile(
   427	                dotfilename=dotfilename,
   428	                colored=graph2use == "colored",
   429	                simple_form=simple_form)
   430	            outfname = format_dot(dotfilename, format=format)
   431	        else:
   432	            graph = self._graph
   433	            if graph2use in ['flat', 'exec']:
   434	                graph = self._create_flat_graph()
   435	            if graph2use == 'exec':
   436	                graph = generate_expanded_graph(deepcopy(graph))
   437	            outfname = export_graph(
   438	                graph,
   439	                base_dir,
   440	                dotfilename=dotfilename,
   441	                format=format,
   442	                simple_form=simple_form)
   443	
   444	        logger.info(
   445	            'Generated workflow graph: %s (graph2use=%s, simple_form=%s).' %
   446	            (outfname, graph2use, simple_form))
   447	        return outfname
   448

   450	                                   dotfilename=None,

   449	    def write_hierarchical_dotfile(self,
   450	                                   dotfilename=None,
   451	                                   colored=False,
   452	                                   simple_form=True):
   453	        dotlist = ['digraph %s{' % self.name]
   454	        dotlist.append(
   455	            self._get_dot(
   456	                prefix='  ', colored=colored, simple_form=simple_form))
   457	        dotlist.append('}')
   458	        dotstr = '\n'.join(dotlist)
   459	        if dotfilename:
   460	            fp = open(dotfilename, 'wt')
   461	            fp.writelines(dotstr)
   462	            fp.close()
   463	        else:
   464	            logger.info(dotstr)
   465	
   466	    def export(self,
   467	               filename=None,
   468	               prefix="output",
   469	               format="python",
   470	               include_config=False):
   471	        """Export object into a different format
   472	
   473	        Parameters
   474	        ----------
   475	        filename: string
   476	           file to save the code to; overrides prefix
   477	        prefix: string
   478	           prefix to use for output file
   479	        format: string
   480	           one of "python"
   481	        include_config: boolean
   482	           whether to include node and workflow config values
   483	
   484	        """
   485	        formats = ["python"]
   486	        if format not in formats:
   487	            raise ValueError('format must be one of: %s' % '|'.join(formats))
   488	        flatgraph = self._create_flat_graph()
   489	        nodes = nx.topological_sort(flatgraph)
   490	
   491	        all_lines = None
   492	        lines = ['# Workflow']
   493	        importlines = [
   494	            'from nipype.pipeline.engine import Workflow, '
   495	            'Node, MapNode'
   496	        ]
   497	        functions = {}
   498	        if format == "python":
   499	            connect_template = '%s.connect(%%s, %%s, %%s, "%%s")' % self.name
   500	            connect_template2 = '%s.connect(%%s, "%%s", %%s, "%%s")' \
   501	                                % self.name
   502	            wfdef = '%s = Workflow("%s")' % (self.name, self.name)
   503	            lines.append(wfdef)
   504	            if include_config:
   505	                lines.append('%s.config = %s' % (self.name, self.config))
   506	            for idx, node in enumerate(nodes):
   507	                nodename = node.fullname.replace('.', '_')
   508	                # write nodes
   509	                nodelines = format_node(
   510	                    node, format='python', include_config=include_config)
   511	                for line in nodelines:
   512	                    if line.startswith('from'):
   513	                        if line not in importlines:
   514	                            importlines.append(line)
   515	                    else:
   516	                        lines.append(line)
   517	                # write connections
   518	                for u, _, d in flatgraph.in_edges(nbunch=node, data=True):
   519	                    for cd in d['connect']:
   520	                        if isinstance(cd[0], tuple):
   521	                            args = list(cd[0])
   522	                            if args[1] in functions:
   523	                                funcname = functions[args[1]]
   524	                            else:
   525	                                func = create_function_from_source(args[1])
   526	                                funcname = [
   527	                                    name for name in func.__globals__
   528	                                    if name != '__builtins__'
   529	                                ][0]
   530	                                functions[args[1]] = funcname
   531	                            args[1] = funcname
   532	                            args = tuple([arg for arg in args if arg])
   533	                            line_args = (u.fullname.replace('.', '_'), args,
   534	                                         nodename, cd[1])
   535	                            line = connect_template % line_args
   536	                            line = line.replace("'%s'" % funcname, funcname)
   537	                            lines.append(line)
   538	                        else:
   539	                            line_args = (u.fullname.replace('.', '_'), cd[0],
   540	                                         nodename, cd[1])
   541	                            lines.append(connect_template2 % line_args)
   542	            functionlines = ['# Functions']
   543	            for function in functions:
   544	                functionlines.append(pickle.loads(function).rstrip())
   545	            all_lines = importlines + functionlines + lines
   546	
   547	            if not filename:
   548	                filename = '%s%s.py' % (prefix, self.name)
   549	            with open(filename, 'wt') as fp:
   550	                fp.writelines('\n'.join(all_lines))

   948	            '#FFFFC8',  # Y

   961	        if level > len(colorset) - 2:
   962	            level = 3  # Loop back to blue
   963	
   964	        dotlist = ['%slabel="%s";' % (prefix, self.name)]
   965	        for node in nx.topological_sort(self._graph):
   966	            fullname = '.'.join(hierarchy + [node.fullname])

   974	                                    'style=filled, color=black, colorscheme'

   977	                else:

   979	                        dotlist.append(
   980	                            ('%s[label="%s", style=filled,'
   981	                             ' fillcolor="%s"];') % (nodename, node_class_name,
   982	                                                     colorset[level]))
   983	                    else:

   985	                                                              node_class_name))

   988	            if isinstance(node, Workflow):

   994	                                   (colorset[level + 1]))

  1002	                        colored=colored,

  1004	                        level=level + 3))

  1019	                                                          subnodename))

   492	def _write_detailed_dot(graph, dotfilename):
   493	    r"""
   494	    Create a dot file with connection info ::
   495	
   496	        digraph structs {
   497	        node [shape=record];
   498	        struct1 [label="<f0> left|<f1> middle|<f2> right"];
   499	        struct2 [label="<f0> one|<f1> two"];
   500	        struct3 [label="hello\nworld |{ b |{c|<here> d|e}| f}| g | h"];
   501	        struct1:f1 -> struct2:f0;
   502	        struct1:f0 -> struct2:f1;
   503	        struct1:f2 -> struct3:here;
   504	        }
   505	    """
   506	    text = ['digraph structs {', 'node [shape=record];']
   507	    # write nodes
   508	    edges = []
   509	    for n in nx.topological_sort(graph):
   510	        nodename = str(n)
   511	        inports = []
   512	        for u, v, d in graph.in_edges(nbunch=n, data=True):
   513	            for cd in d['connect']:
   514	                if isinstance(cd[0], (str, bytes)):
   515	                    outport = cd[0]
   516	                else:
   517	                    outport = cd[0][0]
   518	                inport = cd[1]
   519	                ipstrip = 'in%s' % _replacefunk(inport)
   520	                opstrip = 'out%s' % _replacefunk(outport)
   521	                edges.append(
   522	                    '%s:%s:e -> %s:%s:w;' % (str(u).replace('.', ''), opstrip,
   523	                                             str(v).replace('.', ''), ipstrip))
   524	                if inport not in inports:
   525	                    inports.append(inport)
   526	        inputstr = ['{IN'] + [
   527	            '|<in%s> %s' % (_replacefunk(ip), ip) for ip in sorted(inports)
   528	        ] + ['}']
   529	        outports = []
   530	        for u, v, d in graph.out_edges(nbunch=n, data=True):
   531	            for cd in d['connect']:
   532	                if isinstance(cd[0], (str, bytes)):
   533	                    outport = cd[0]
   534	                else:
   535	                    outport = cd[0][0]
   536	                if outport not in outports:
   537	                    outports.append(outport)
   538	        outputstr = ['{OUT'] + [
   539	            '|<out%s> %s' % (_replacefunk(oport), oport)
   540	            for oport in sorted(outports)
   541	        ] + ['}']
   542	        srcpackage = ''
   543	        if hasattr(n, '_interface'):
   544	            pkglist = n.interface.__class__.__module__.split('.')
   545	            if len(pkglist) > 2:
   546	                srcpackage = pkglist[2]
   547	        srchierarchy = '.'.join(nodename.split('.')[1:-1])
   548	        nodenamestr = '{ %s | %s | %s }' % (nodename.split('.')[-1],
   549	                                            srcpackage, srchierarchy)
   550	        text += [
</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/nipype/pipeline/engine/utils.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[550, 580]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/nipype/pipeline/engine/utils.py:

   553	             ''.join(outputstr))
   554	        ]
   555	    # write edges

   558	    text.append('}')
   559	    with open(dotfilename, 'wt') as filep:
   560	        filep.write('\n'.join(text))

   564	def _replacefunk(x):
   565	    return x.replace('_', '').replace('.', '').replace('@', '').replace(
   566	        '-', '')

   570	def _get_valid_pathstr(pathstr):
   571	    """Remove disallowed characters from path
   572	
   573	    Removes:  [][ (){}?:<>#!|"';]
   574	    Replaces: ',' -> '.'
   575	    """
   576	    if not isinstance(pathstr, (str, bytes)):
   577	        pathstr = to_str(pathstr)
   578	    pathstr = pathstr.replace(os.sep, '..')
   579	    pathstr = re.sub(r'''[][ (){}?:<>#!|"';]''', '', pathstr)
   580	    pathstr = pathstr.replace(',', '.')

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/nipype/pipeline/engine/utils.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[447, 490]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/nipype/pipeline/engine/utils.py:
   447	def get_print_name(node, simple_form=True):
   448	    """Get the name of the node
   449	
   450	    For example, a node containing an instance of interfaces.fsl.BET
   451	    would be called nodename.BET.fsl
   452	
   453	    """
   454	    name = node.fullname
   455	    if hasattr(node, '_interface'):
   456	        pkglist = node.interface.__class__.__module__.split('.')
   457	        interface = node.interface.__class__.__name__
   458	        destclass = ''
   459	        if len(pkglist) > 2:
   460	            destclass = '.%s' % pkglist[2]
   461	        if simple_form:
   462	            name = node.fullname + destclass
   463	        else:
   464	            name = '.'.join([node.fullname, interface]) + destclass
   465	    if simple_form:
   466	        parts = name.split('.')
   467	        if len(parts) > 2:
   468	            return ' ('.join(parts[1:]) + ')'
   469	        elif len(parts) == 2:
   470	            return parts[1]
   471	    return name
   472	
   473	
   474	def _create_dot_graph(graph, show_connectinfo=False, simple_form=True):
   475	    """Create a graph that can be pickled.
   476	
   477	    Ensures that edge info is pickleable.
   478	    """
   479	    logger.debug('creating dot graph')
   480	    pklgraph = nx.DiGraph()
   481	    for edge in graph.edges():
   482	        data = graph.get_edge_data(*edge)
   483	        srcname = get_print_name(edge[0], simple_form=simple_form)
   484	        destname = get_print_name(edge[1], simple_form=simple_form)
   485	        if show_connectinfo:
   486	            pklgraph.add_edge(srcname, destname, l=str(data['connect']))
   487	        else:
   488	            pklgraph.add_edge(srcname, destname)
   489	    return pklgraph

"""
Debug script to understand node representation issues.

import os
import tempfile
from nipype.pipeline.engine import Workflow, Node
from nipype.interfaces.utility import IdentityInterface, Function
import nipype.interfaces.io as nio

def debug_node_representation():
    """Debug what str() returns for nodes."""
    
    # Create workflow
    wf = Workflow(name='test_workflow')
    
    # Create some nodes
    inputnode = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='inputnode')
    process1 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                           function=lambda x: x + 1), name='process1')
    process2 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                           function=lambda x: x * 2), name='process2')
    outputnode = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['final_result']), name='outputnode')
    
    # Print various representations
    print("=== Node Representation Debug ===")
    print(f"inputnode.name: {inputnode.name}")
    print(f"inputnode.fullname: {inputnode.fullname}")
    print(f"str(inputnode): {str(inputnode)}")
    print(f"repr(inputnode): {repr(inputnode)}")
    print()
    
    print(f"process1.name: {process1.name}")
    print(f"process1.fullname: {process1.fullname}")
    print(f"str(process1): {str(process1)}")
    print(f"repr(process1): {repr(process1)}")
    print()
    
    # Connect nodes to workflow
    wf.add_nodes([inputnode, process1, process2, outputnode])
    
    print("=== After adding to workflow ===")
    print(f"inputnode.name: {inputnode.name}")
    print(f"inputnode.fullname: {inputnode.fullname}")
    print(f"str(inputnode): {str(inputnode)}")
    print()
    
    # Connect nodes
    wf.connect([
        (inputnode, process1, [('data', 'data')]),
        (process1, process2, [('result', 'data')]),
        (process2, outputnode, [('result', 'final_result')])
    ])
    
    print("=== After connecting nodes ===")
    print(f"inputnode.name: {inputnode.name}")
    print(f"inputnode.fullname: {inputnode.fullname}")
    print(f"str(inputnode): {str(inputnode)}")
    print()
    
    # Check what happens in _write_detailed_dot
    print("=== Simulating _write_detailed_dot behavior ===")
    graph = wf._graph
    for n in graph.nodes():
        nodename = str(n)
        print(f"Node: {n}")
        print(f"  str(n): '{nodename}'")
        print(f"  str(n).replace('.', ''): '{nodename.replace('.', '')}'")
        print()

def create_complex_workflow():
    """Create a more complex workflow to better reproduce the issue."""
    
    # Main workflow
    main_wf = Workflow(name='main_workflow')
    
    # Sub-workflow
    sub_wf = Workflow(name='sub_workflow')
    
    # Nodes in main workflow
    input_main = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='input_main')
    process_main = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x + 1), name='process_main')
    
    # Nodes in sub-workflow
    input_sub = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='input_sub')
    process_sub1 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x * 2), name='process_sub1')
    process_sub2 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x - 1), name='process_sub2')
    
    # Add nodes to sub-workflow
    sub_wf.add_nodes([input_sub, process_sub1, process_sub2])
    sub_wf.connect(input_sub, 'data', process_sub1, 'data')
    sub_wf.connect(process_sub1, 'result', process_sub2, 'data')
    
    # Add nodes to main workflow
    main_wf.add_nodes([input_main, process_main, sub_wf])
    main_wf.connect(input_main, 'data', process_main, 'data')
    main_wf.connect(process_main, 'result', sub_wf, 'input_sub.data')
    
    return main_wf

In the detailed graph, the nodes are:
- `"input_main (utility)"`
- `"process_main (utility)"`
- `"sub_workflow (input_sub (utility)"`
- `"sub_workflow (process_sub1 (utility)"`
- `"sub_workflow (process_sub2 (utility)"`

Notice that the last three entries have malformed names:
- They start with `"sub_workflow (` but don't close the parenthesis properly
- It should be `"sub_workflow.input_sub (utility)"` but instead it's `"sub_workflow (input_sub (utility)"`

This is because in the `_write_detailed_dot` function, the code is doing:
1. `nodename = str(n)` which gives something like `'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub'`
2. Then `nodename.replace('.', '')` which removes ALL dots, turning it into `'main_workflowsub_workflowinput_sub'`
3. But then in the `get_print_name` function, it adds the `(utility)` part

However, the issue is that the `nodename.replace('.', '')` is removing dots from the wrong place. Let me trace this more carefully by looking at the actual code.

1. Line 510: `nodename = str(n)` gets the full node name like `'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub'`
2. Line 547: `srchierarchy = '.'.join(nodename.split('.')[1:-1])` tries to extract the hierarchy 
3. Line 548: `nodename.split('.')[-1]` gets the actual node name
4. Line 549: These are assembled into `nodenamestr`
5. Line 552: `nodename.replace('.', '')` removes ALL dots from the original nodename for the DOT node ID

The problem is in line 547 and 548. Let me trace what happens with `'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub'`:

- `nodename.split('.')` = `['main_workflow', 'sub_workflow', 'input_sub']`
- `nodename.split('.')[1:-1]` = `['sub_workflow']` (middle elements)
- `nodename.split('.')[-1]` = `'input_sub'` (last element)
- So `srchierarchy` = `'sub_workflow'` and the node name = `'input_sub'`

But then in line 548, `nodenamestr` becomes:
`'{ input_sub | utility | sub_workflow }'`

This gets formatted into the label, and when combined with the parentheses from `get_print_name`, it creates malformed labels.

Wait, let me check how `get_print_name` is used here. Actually, looking at the code, `get_print_name` is NOT used in `_write_detailed_dot`. Instead, the package name is extracted directly and the hierarchy is manually constructed.

Let me look at what `get_print_name` produces vs what's happening here:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/debug_get_print_name.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python

def debug_different_approaches():
    """Compare get_print_name vs _write_detailed_dot approach."""
    
    # Create workflow with nested structure
    main_wf = Workflow(name='main_workflow')
    sub_wf = Workflow(name='sub_workflow')
    
    input_sub = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='input_sub')
    process_sub1 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x * 2), name='process_sub1')
    
    # Add nodes to sub-workflow
    sub_wf.add_nodes([input_sub, process_sub1])
    sub_wf.connect(input_sub, 'data', process_sub1, 'data')
    
    # Add sub-workflow to main
    main_wf.add_nodes([sub_wf])
    
    # Get the actual node objects from the graph
    graph = main_wf._create_flat_graph()

    for n in graph.nodes():
        print(f"\nNode: {n}")
        print(f"  str(n): '{str(n)}'")
        print(f"  get_print_name(n, simple_form=True): '{get_print_name(n, simple_form=True)}'")
        print(f"  get_print_name(n, simple_form=False): '{get_print_name(n, simple_form=False)}'")
        
        # Simulate _write_detailed_dot logic
        nodename = str(n)
        print(f"  nodename: '{nodename}'")
        print(f"  nodename.split('.'): {nodename.split('.')}")
        print(f"  nodename.split('.')[1:-1]: {nodename.split('.')[1:-1]}")

            srchierarchy = '.'.join(nodename.split('.')[1:-1])
            actual_name = nodename.split('.')[-1]
            print(f"  srchierarchy: '{srchierarchy}'")
            print(f"  actual_name: '{actual_name}'")

            srcpackage = ''
            if hasattr(n, '_interface'):
                pkglist = n.interface.__class__.__module__.split('.')
                if len(pkglist) > 2:
                    srcpackage = pkglist[2]
            print(f"  srcpackage: '{srcpackage}'")
            
            nodenamestr = '{ %s | %s | %s }' % (actual_name, srcpackage, srchierarchy)
            print(f"  nodenamestr: '{nodenamestr}'")
            
            # Final label construction (as in _write_detailed_dot)
            final_label = '"%s|%s|%s"' % (''.join(['{IN}']), nodenamestr, ''.join(['{OUT}']))
            print(f"  final_label: {final_label}")
        else:
            print(f"  Simple case - less than 3 parts")

For a node with `fullname = 'main.sub.node1'` and `destclass = '.utility'`:

1. Line 462: `name = 'main.sub.node1.utility'`
2. Line 466: `parts = ['main', 'sub', 'node1', 'utility']`
3. Line 468: `' ('.join(parts[1:]) + ')'` = `' ('.join(['sub', 'node1', 'utility']) + ')'` = `'sub (node1 (utility' + ')'` = `'sub (node1 (utility)'`

This is clearly wrong! The intention is not to join ALL parts with `' ('`, but to format it properly.

Looking at the docstring and the expected behavior, the desired format seems to be:
- Take everything after the first part (`sub.node1`)
- Add the package in parentheses (`sub.node1 (utility)`)

But the current implementation tries to join with `' ('` which creates malformed strings.

def test_various_cases():

- `destclass = '.utility'` 
- `name = 'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub.utility'`
- `parts = ['main_workflow', 'sub_workflow', 'input_sub', 'utility']`
- `' ('.join(parts[1:]) + ')'` = `' ('.join(['sub_workflow', 'input_sub', 'utility']) + ')'` = `'sub_workflow (input_sub (utility' + ')'` = `'sub_workflow (input_sub (utility)'`

That's definitely wrong! The issue is the join operation.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The problem is in the `get_print_name` function in `/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/nipype/pipeline/engine/utils.py` at line 468. When dealing with deeply nested workflows (3+ levels), the function incorrectly joins the parts with `' ('`, leading to malformed node names like `'sub_workflow (input_sub (utility)'` instead of the expected `'sub_workflow.input_sub (utility)'`.

### 5.2 Problem Location
File: `/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/nipype/pipeline/engine/utils.py`
Function: `get_print_name`
Line: 468
Code: `return ' ('.join(parts[1:]) + ')'`

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
My test creates a workflow hierarchy with `main_workflow -> sub_workflow -> nodes`, resulting in node fullnames like `'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub'`. When the package info `.utility` is appended, we get `'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub.utility'`. The buggy join logic then produces `'sub_workflow (input_sub (utility)'` instead of `'sub_workflow.input_sub (utility)'`.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Maintain backward compatibility 
- Fix only the specific joining logic
- Ensure the fix works for all nesting levels (1, 2, 3+ levels)
- Preserve the intended format where the package name is in parentheses

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem
Instead of joining all parts with `' ('`, we should:
1. Take all parts except the first (workflow name) and the last (package)
2. Join them with `'.'` to preserve the hierarchy 
3. Add the package name in parentheses

So for `'main_workflow.sub_workflow.input_sub.utility'`:
- Parts: `['main_workflow', 'sub_workflow', 'input_sub', 'utility']`  
- Take `parts[1:-1]` = `['sub_workflow', 'input_sub']` → `'sub_workflow.input_sub'`
- Add package: `'sub_workflow.input_sub (utility)'`

def test_all_levels():
    """Test node naming at different nesting levels."""
    
    print("=== Comprehensive Fix Test ===")
    
    # Level 1: Direct node in workflow

    inner2 = Workflow(name='inner2')

            hier_file = outer3.write_graph(

                format='dot',
                simple_form=True

                hier_content = f.read()
                print("Hierarchical graph content:")
                # Extract just the node labels
                import re
                labels = re.findall(r'label="([^"]+)"', hier_content)
                for label in labels:
                    print(f"  Node label: '{label}'")

            print(f"Error with hierarchical graph: {e}")

        try:

                # Filter out edge definitions

                for node_name in node_names:
                    print(f"  Node name: '{node_name}'")
                    
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error with detailed graph: {e}")

def create_proper_deep_hierarchy():
    """Create a proper deep hierarchy and test it."""
    
    print("=== Proper Deep Hierarchy Test ===")
    
    # Create workflows bottom-up
    level1_wf = Workflow(name='level1')

    return deep_node, simple_name

"""
Test the real deep case that appears in flat graphs.
"""

from nipype.pipeline.engine import Workflow, Node
from nipype.interfaces.utility import IdentityInterface
from nipype.pipeline.engine.utils import get_print_name

def test_real_deep_case():
    """Test the real deep case from flat graph."""
    
    print("=== Real Deep Case Test ===")
    
    # Create the hierarchy
    level1_wf = Workflow(name='level1')
    level2_wf = Workflow(name='level2') 
    level3_wf = Workflow(name='level3')
    deep_node = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='deep_node')
    
    # Build proper hierarchy
    level3_wf.add_nodes([deep_node])
    level2_wf.add_nodes([level3_wf])
    level1_wf.add_nodes([level2_wf])
    
    # Get flat graph to see the real fullname
    flat_graph = level1_wf._create_flat_graph()
    
    # Find our node in the flat graph
    target_node = None
    for node in flat_graph.nodes():
        if 'deep_node' in str(node):
            target_node = node
            break
    
    if target_node:
        print(f"Found node in flat graph: '{target_node}'")
        
        # Mock the node to have the fullname from flat graph
        # (In reality, get_print_name would be called on the flat graph node)
        class MockNode:
            def __init__(self, fullname):
                self.fullname = fullname
                self._interface = deep_node._interface
                
        mock_node = MockNode(str(target_node))
        result = get_print_name(mock_node, simple_form=True)
        
        print(f"get_print_name result: '{result}'")
        
        # What we expect:
        # fullname: 'level1.level2.level3.deep_node'
        # with package: 'level1.level2.level3.deep_node.utility'  
        # parts: ['level1', 'level2', 'level3', 'deep_node', 'utility']
        # expected: 'level2.level3.deep_node (utility)'
        
        parts = str(target_node).split('.') + ['utility']
        expected_hierarchy = '.'.join(parts[1:-1])  # level2.level3.deep_node
        expected_package = parts[-1]  # utility
        expected = '{} ({})'.format(expected_hierarchy, expected_package)
        
        print(f"Expected: '{expected}'")
        print(f"Match: {result == expected}")
    else:
        print("Node not found in flat graph")

def test_with_actual_graph_gen():
    """Test with actual graph generation that was problematic."""
    
    print("\n=== Actual Graph Generation Test ===")
    
    # Recreate the problematic case from our earlier test
    main_wf = Workflow(name='main_workflow')
    sub_wf = Workflow(name='sub_workflow')
    
    input_sub = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='input_sub')
    process_sub1 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x * 2), name='process_sub1')
    process_sub2 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x - 1), name='process_sub2')
    
    sub_wf.add_nodes([input_sub, process_sub1, process_sub2])
    sub_wf.connect(input_sub, 'data', process_sub1, 'data')
    sub_wf.connect(process_sub1, 'result', process_sub2, 'data')
    
    main_wf.add_nodes([sub_wf])
    
    # Test get_print_name directly on the nodes
    print("Testing get_print_name on sub-workflow nodes:")
    graph = main_wf._create_flat_graph()
    
    for node in graph.nodes():
        if 'sub_workflow' in str(node):
            print(f"  Node: '{node}'")
            # Create mock node since we can't directly call get_print_name on graph nodes
            class MockNode:
                def __init__(self, fullname, interface):
                    self.fullname = fullname
                    self._interface = interface
                    
            mock_node = MockNode(str(node), list(graph.nodes())[0]._interface)  # borrow interface
            result = get_print_name(mock_node, simple_form=True)
            print(f"    get_print_name: '{result}'")
    
    # Generate the actual graphs to see if our fix worked
    import tempfile
    import os
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        main_wf.base_dir = tmpdir
        
        # Generate detailed graph (this was the problematic one)
        detail_file = main_wf.write_graph(
            dotfilename=os.path.join(tmpdir, 'detail_check.dot'),
            graph2use='flat',
            format='dot',
            simple_form=True
        )
        
        with open(detail_file, 'r') as f:
            content = f.read()
            print("\nDetailed graph node names:")
            import re
            # Extract quoted strings that look like node names (not edges)
            matches = re.findall(r'"([^"]+)"', content)
            node_names = [m for m in matches if '->' not in m]
            for name in node_names:
                print(f"  '{name}'")
                # Check if it looks correct (should not have malformed parentheses)
                if '(' in name and ')' in name:
                    paren_content = name[name.find('(')+1:name.find(')')]
                    if '(' in paren_content or ')' in paren_content:
                        print(f"    WARNING: Malformed parentheses detected!")
                    else:
                        print(f"    OK: Proper parentheses format")

"""
Simple test focusing on the actual graph generation that was fixed.
"""

from nipype.pipeline.engine import Workflow, Node
from nipype.interfaces.utility import IdentityInterface, Function
import tempfile
import os
import re

def test_fixed_graph_generation():
    """Test that the graph generation now works correctly."""
    
    print("=== Testing Fixed Graph Generation ===")
    
    # Recreate the exact problematic case from our earlier findings
    main_wf = Workflow(name='main_workflow')
    sub_wf = Workflow(name='sub_workflow')
    
    input_sub = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='input_sub')
    process_sub1 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x * 2), name='process_sub1')
    process_sub2 = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x - 1), name='process_sub2')
    
    sub_wf.add_nodes([input_sub, process_sub1, process_sub2])
    sub_wf.connect(input_sub, 'data', process_sub1, 'data')
    sub_wf.connect(process_sub1, 'result', process_sub2, 'data')
    
    main_wf.add_nodes([sub_wf])
    
    # Generate graphs and check the output
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        main_wf.base_dir = tmpdir
        
        # Test 1: Hierarchical graph
        print("1. Testing hierarchical graph...")
        hier_file = main_wf.write_graph(
            dotfilename=os.path.join(tmpdir, 'hierarchical_test.dot'),
            graph2use='hierarchical',
            format='dot',
            simple_form=True
        )
        
        with open(hier_file, 'r') as f:
            hier_content = f.read()
            print("   Generated hierarchical graph content:")
            print(hier_content)
        
        # Test 2: Detailed (flat) graph - this was the problematic one
        print("\n2. Testing detailed graph (THE FIX)...")
        detail_file = main_wf.write_graph(
            dotfilename=os.path.join(tmpdir, 'detailed_test.dot'),
            graph2use='flat',
            format='dot',
            simple_form=True
        )
        
        with open(detail_file, 'r') as f:
            detail_content = f.read()
            print("   Generated detailed graph content:")
            print(detail_content)
            
            # Analyze the node names
            print("\n   Analysis of node names:")
            matches = re.findall(r'"([^"]+)"', detail_content)
            node_names = [m for m in matches if '->' not in m and len(m.strip()) > 0]
            
            for i, name in enumerate(node_names):
                print(f"     Node {i+1}: '{name}'")
                
                # Check for proper parentheses formatting
                if '(' in name and ')' in name:
                    # Extract content between parentheses
                    start_paren = name.find('(')
                    end_paren = name.find(')')
                    if start_paren < end_paren:
                        paren_content = name[start_paren+1:end_paren]
                        # Check if parentheses content contains more parentheses (malformed)
                        if '(' not in paren_content and ')' not in paren_content:
                            print(f"       ✓ OK: Proper parentheses format")
                        else:
                            print(f"       ✗ ERROR: Malformed parentheses!")
                            return False
                    else:
                        print(f"       ✗ ERROR: Mismatched parentheses!")
                        return False
                else:
                    print(f"       Info: No parentheses (may be workflow)")
            
            print(f"\n   ✓ All node names appear to be correctly formatted!")
            return True

def compare_before_after():
    """Show what the output looked like before vs after the fix."""
    
    print("\n=== Before vs After Comparison ===")
    print("BEFORE (buggy output):")
    print('  "sub_workflow (input_sub (utility)";')
    print('  "sub_workflow (process_sub1 (utility)";')
    print('  "sub_workflow (process_sub2 (utility)";')
    print("  Issues: Missing closing parenthesis, malformed names")
    
    print("\nAFTER (fixed output):")
    # We'll generate this to show current output
    main_wf = Workflow(name='main_workflow')
    sub_wf = Workflow(name='sub_workflow')
    test_node = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='test_node')
    
    sub_wf.add_nodes([test_node])
    main_wf.add_nodes([sub_wf])
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        main_wf.base_dir = tmpdir
        detail_file = main_wf.write_graph(
            dotfilename=os.path.join(tmpdir, 'comparison.dot'),
            graph2use='flat',
            format='dot',
            simple_form=True
        )
        
        with open(detail_file, 'r') as f:
            content = f.read()
            matches = re.findall(r'"([^"]+)"', content)
            node_names = [m for m in matches if '->' not in m and len(m.strip()) > 0]
            
            for name in node_names:
                if 'test_node' in name:
                    print(f'  "{name}";')

if __name__ == '__main__':

    compare_before_after()

Let me create one final test to demonstrate that the fix works:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/final_verification.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""
Final verification that the fix for incorrect detailed graphs is working.
This reproduces the exact issue described in the GitHub issue.
"""

import tempfile
import os
from nipype.pipeline.engine import Workflow, Node
from nipype.interfaces.utility import IdentityInterface, Function

def test_fix():
    """Test that the fix resolves the graph naming issue."""
    
    print("=== Testing Fix for Incorrect Detailed Graphs ===")
    
    # Create a nested workflow structure that would trigger the bug
    main_wf = Workflow(name='main_workflow')
    sub_wf = Workflow(name='sub_workflow')
    
    # Create nodes
    input_node = Node(IdentityInterface(fields=['data']), name='input_node')
    process_node = Node(Function(input_names=['data'], output_names=['result'], 
                               function=lambda x: x * 2), name='process_node')
    
    # Build hierarchy
    sub_wf.add_nodes([input_node, process_node])
    sub_wf.connect(input_node, 'data', process_node, 'data')
    main_wf.add_nodes([sub_wf])
    
    # Test graph generation
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        main_wf.base_dir = tmpdir
        
        # Generate detailed (flat) graph - this was the problematic case
        graph_file = main_wf.write_graph(
            dotfilename=os.path.join(tmpdir, 'test_detailed.dot'),
            graph2use='flat',
            format='dot',
            simple_form=True
        )
        
        # Read and analyze the output
        with open(graph_file, 'r') as f:
            content = f.read()
            
        print("Generated detailed graph content:")
        print(content)
        
        # Check for proper node naming
        import re
        node_names = re.findall(r'"([^"]+)"', content)
        node_names = [n for n in node_names if '->' not in n]  # Filter out edges
        
        print("\nNode names found:")
        all_correct = True
        for name in node_names:
            if 'input_node' in name or 'process_node' in name:
                print(f"  '{name}'")
                # Check for proper parentheses format
                if name.count('(') != name.count(')'):
                    print(f"    ❌ ERROR: Mismatched parentheses!")
                    all_correct = False
                elif '(' in name and ')' in name:
                    paren_content = name[name.find('(')+1:name.find(')')]
                    if '(' in paren_content or ')' in paren_content:
                        print(f"    ❌ ERROR: Nested/malformed parentheses!")
                        all_correct = False
                    else:
                        print(f"    ✅ OK: Proper parentheses format")
                else:
                    print(f"    ℹ️  Info: No package info (may be workflow)")
        
        if all_correct:
            print(f"\n🎉 SUCCESS: All node names have correct formatting!")
            print("The fix for incorrect detailed graphs is working properly.")
        else:
            print(f"\n❌ FAILURE: Some node names still have formatting issues.")
            
        return all_correct

if __name__ == '__main__':
    success = test_fix()
    exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1/final_verification.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.1 && python final_verification.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>=== Testing Fix for Incorrect Detailed Graphs ===
